Model-dependent limits are computed for the various signal scenarios considered in the analysis. The hypothesis tests are performed including the expected signal yield and its associated uncertainties in the CRs and SRs. All limits are quoted at 95% CL with the CL
s
method. When setting limits, the two-body selection binned SRs
\mathrm{SR-DF}^{\mathrm{2-body}}_{[x,y)}
and
\mathrm{SR-SF}^{\mathrm{2-body}}_{[x,y)}
regions are combined. Similarly, the SR-DF
{}^{\mathrm{3-body}}_{W}
, SR-SF
{}^{\mathrm{3-body}}_{W}
, SR-DF
{}^{\mathrm{3-body}}_{t}
, and SR-SF
{}^{\mathrm{3-body}}_{t}
signal regions are combined for the three-body selection, and so are SR
{}^{\mathrm{4-body}}_{\mathrm{Small}\,\Delta m}
and SR
{}^{\mathrm{4-body}}_{\mathrm{Large}\,\Delta m}
for the four-body selection.

Limits for simplified models in which pair-produced
\tilde{t}_{1}
decay with 100% branching ratio into a top quark and
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
are shown in the
\tilde{t}_{1}
–
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
mass plane in Figure
14
and in the
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
–
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
plane in Figure
14
.
The exclusion contour is the envelope of the exclusion regions obtained separately for the three selections.
Top squark masses up to 1
TeV
are excluded for a massless lightest neutralino. Neutralino masses up to 500
GeV
are excluded for
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
above the top quark production kinematic limit. In the three-body decay region, top squark masses are excluded up to 600
GeV
for
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=120\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
, up to
550\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
for
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
close to the top quark mass and up to 430
GeV
for
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
close to the
W
boson mass. In the four-body decay region, top squark masses are excluded up to 540
GeV
for
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=40\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
. Top squark decay around the
W
boson production kinematic limit is not fully excluded for
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
above 400
GeV
because there the four-body and three-body decay exclusion regions do not overlap. The four-body selection loses sensitivity for
\Delta m(\tilde{t},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})\gtrsim m(W)
due to the upper bound of the sub-leading lepton
p_{\text{T}}
while, for the three body selection, the
M_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{R}}
requirement suppresses the sensitivity for
\Delta m(\tilde{t},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})\lesssim m(W)
because of the smaller mass splitting. The three-body and two-body overlap in the sensitivity provides exclusion coverage around the top quark production kinematic limit up to
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
of 540
GeV
.

Figure 14:
Exclusion limit contour (95% CL) for a simplified model assuming
\tilde{t}_{1}
pair production, decaying via
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow t^{(*)}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
with 100% branching ratio, in the (a)
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
–
m(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
and (b)
m(\tilde{t}_{1})
–
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
planes. The dashed lines and the shaded bands are the expected limits and their
\pm 1\sigma
uncertainties.
The thick solid lines are the observed limits for the central value of the signal cross-section. The expected and observed limits do not include the effect of the theoretical uncertainties in the signal cross-section. The dotted lines show the effect on the observed limit when varying the signal cross-section by
\pm 1\sigma
of the theoretical uncertainty.

For the DM mediator models, Figure
15
shows upper limits at 95% CL on the observed signal cross-section scaled to the theoretical signal cross-section for a coupling
g=g_{q}=g_{\chi}=1
, denoted by
\sigma_{\mathrm{obs}}/\sigma_{\mathrm{Th}}(g=1.0)
.
These limits are obtained as a function of the mediator mass, assuming a specific DM particle mass of
1\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
. Both the scalar and pseudoscalar mediator cases are considered.
The sensitivity is approximately constant for mediator masses below
100\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
and the models are excluded for scalar (pseudoscalar) mediator masses up to
250\penalty\ (300)\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
when assuming
g=1
.

Figure 15:
Exclusion limits for (a)
t\bar{t}+\phi
scalar and (b)
t\bar{t}+a
pseudoscalar models as a function of the mediator mass for a DM particle mass of
m(\chi)=1\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
. The limits are calculated at 95% CL and are expressed in terms of the ratio of the excluded cross-section to the nominal cross-section for a coupling assumption of
g=g_{q}=g_{\chi}=1
. The solid (dashed) lines shows the observed (expected) exclusion limits.

10
Conclusion

This paper reports the results of a search for direct top squark pair production and for dark matter in a final state containing two leptons with opposite electric charge, jets and missing transverse momentum.
The search uses an integrated luminosity of
139\penalty\ \mbox{fb${}^{-1}$}
of proton–proton collisions at
\sqrt{s}=13\penalty\ \text{Te\kern-1.00006ptV}
, as collected by the ATLAS experiment at the Large Hadron Collider during Run 2 (2015–2018).

Compared to previous searches a significant improvement in sensitivity is obtained by using additional integrated luminosity and a new discriminating variable, the object-based
E_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{miss}}\ \mathrm{significance}
.
Moreover, in the small-
\Delta m(\tilde{t}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
region, an important gain in sensitivity is also achieved by lowering the
p_{\text{T}}
threshold for lepton selection.

The data are found to be consistent with the Standard Model predictions. Assuming direct
\tilde{t}_{1}
pair production
with both top squarks decaying in either the two-body channel
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, the three-body channel
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow bW\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, or the four-body channel
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow b\ell\nu\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, constraints at 95% confidence level are placed on the minimum
\tilde{t}_{1}
and
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
masses up to about 1
TeV
and
500\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
respectively. The results improve on the previous ATLAS limits obtained in a two-lepton final state and provide unique sensitivity among the ATLAS searches in the mass region where the decay
\tilde{t}_{1}\rightarrow t\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
becomes kinematically allowed. For the dark-matter model, assuming spin-0 mediator production in association with a pair of top quarks and decay with 100% branching ratio into a pair of dark-matter particles, scalar (pseudoscalar) mediator masses up to about
250\penalty\ (300)\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
are excluded at 95% confidence level for mediator couplings
g_{q}=g_{\chi}=1
to Standard Model and dark-matter particles.
